| [S23](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#m-S23) | The Shareholder Revolt     | 19    | Black Card | [Creation & Company](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#u-cco)          | The Minutes of Removal             |
| [S24](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#m-S24) | Awaiting Signature         | 20    | Black Card | [Creation & Company](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#u-cco)          | —                                  |

Characters level up after the mission shown at the next level, or after equivalent side contracts. The party should reach 20th level at the start of S24.

Chapter 3

## Brandmere, City of Franchises

**Brandmere** is a harbour city of two hundred thousand souls built on a river delta, where three trade roads meet the sea. It was an ordinary market town until, about a century ago, it became the first city in the realm to grant franchise charters. Now it is the commercial capital of the world: every chain has a flagship here, every holding company a tower, and every street a loyalty scheme. The city's motto, carved over the Old Gate, reads *Fides et Mercatura*—“Trust and Trade.” Someone has since added a ™.

### Brandmere at a Glance

| **Government** | The Chamber of Commerce, a council of franchise owners weighted by market share. The Lord Mayor, Esmond Tallow-Pryce, is a figurehead who does not realise his office has been leased.                    |
| -------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Law**        | The City Watch, most of whom are paid in Dungeon Donuts vouchers. Serious crimes are resolved by the Arbitration Court on Guild Row, where both sides pay a filing fee and the higher fee wins most ties. |
| **Currency**   | Standard coin, plus loyalty points, which are accepted in more shops than gold. Loyalty points cannot be converted back.                                                                                  |
| **News**       | *The Brandmere Crier* (independent, tiny, brave) and *The Market Herald* (owned by McGuffin Narrative Holdings, very large, always has a twist).                                                          |
| **Religion**   | The old temples still stand but rent their forecourts to kiosks. In Act III the Temple of the Healing Hand is sued for trademark infringement (S12).                                                      |

### Districts

#### Guild Row

Offices, courts and the **Warren**, QuestRabbit's cheerful burrow-tower, whose job boards glow when a contract posts. Harriet Hopwell's office is on the top floor; Pip Ashworth's desk is in the lobby beside a ficus. The Arbitration Court and the Guild of Scriveners are across the square.

**Hook.** A contractor who hasn't been seen in a month still has a job listed as “in progress.” Pip is worried.

#### Drive-Thru Row

A mile of fast-food flagships under hanging signs, open all night: THAC0 Spell, Murder King, Wight Castle, Five Wights, the Brandmere Grand Tusks. Carts queue in the road. The THAC0 Spell flagship, managed by Hollis Quench, is where the campaign begins, and where the Franchise Below lies waiting.

**Hook.** A drive-thru speaking-tube has started taking orders from someone who isn't there.

#### Corporate Row

The towers. Crown & Cleaver's black-glass spire, the Arcane Beverage International Reserve Roastery, the Hoard Syndicate's vault-bank. Upmarket franchises serve people who never eat anywhere else. Every building has a lobby with a fountain and a receptionist with a clipboard.

**Hook.** Every clock on Corporate Row stopped at the same minute. The towers want it kept quiet.

#### The Old Independents' Quarter

Crooked streets, family taverns and shops that have refused every franchise agreement. The heart is **Tallow Lane** and the **Tankard & Tallow**, Marda Hobb's tavern and the unofficial headquarters of the Last Independents. By Act V it's the party's war room. The Quarter shrinks every year; people who sign franchise deals here tend to vanish.

**Hook.** Marda asks the party to walk a new baker home each night. No payment. (Commons Tally.)

#### The Harbour & Driftwood Quarter

Docks, fish markets and the Codfather's Harbour Fry. Beyond the breakwater, a district sank when the coast flooded forty years ago; its rooftops show at low tide, and a Golden Tusks sign still glows beneath the water (S21). The Driftwood Quarter's taverns cater to sailors and sahuagin traders.

**Hook.** A letter arrives, dated tomorrow, postmarked from below the harbour.

#### The Songbird Quarter

Music halls, bard bars and all-night cafés: Hard Bard Café, Bard to the Bone, Wok & Roll Initiative. Street performers need a licence from McGuffin Narrative Holdings, which owns the phrase “once upon a time.”

**Hook.** Every busker in the Quarter is playing the same song, and none of them wrote it.

#### The Rows

A grid of specialty streets named for trades—Adventurers' Row, Brewer's Row, Tinkers' Row, Stonewall Row, Gamblers' Row—each dominated by a franchise that pushed out the craft it's named for. Probability Brands Group owns most of Gamblers' Row. The dice are loaded. So is the rent.

**Hook.** The last independent blacksmith on Adventurers' Row has been offered a sum of money that is exactly his lifetime earnings to the copper.

#### The Retail Park

Outside the old walls, on land that used to be farms: the Wand-Mart Supercentre, Quest Buy, the CostCo-op warehouse and the IKEA Mimic showroom, whose car park is larger than some villages. Caravans come from three countries to buy in bulk. Nobody who works nights here talks about the Showroom.

**Hook.** The Retail Park has a new aisle. It wasn't there yesterday, and it's longer on the inside.

#### Camp Brandmere & the Tollgate Pass

The fortified depot of the Little Caesars Legion, which treats pizza delivery as a military occupation. Beyond it, the Tollgate Pass climbs into the mountains, where the Summit Tusks franchise sits on a peak that sees every road in the realm.

**Hook.** The Legion has begun “delivering” to villages that didn't order anything.

#### Planar Quay

A dock that isn't on the river. Portals to the City of Brass, Mechanus and the Astral are leased by the Planar Hospitality Group, with customs officers who check both your luggage and your soul. Trader Djinn's and Plane Inn keep flagships here.

**Hook.** Customs has impounded a crate addressed to the party. It is ticking, in a language.

### Recurring Faces

| NPCRoleArc               |                                                                |                                                                                                                                               |
| ------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Pip Ashworth**         | Harengon QuestRabbit liaison; the party's handler              | Genuinely kind; learns the truth in Act IV; walks into the Signing Room holding his own Clause 77-B (S24).                                    |
| **Harriet Hopwell**      | Founder and CEO of QuestRabbit                                 | Believes in her company. Sold it ninety-five years ago without knowing to whom. Can revoke the party's rating at the AGM (S20).               |
| **Hollis Quench**        | THAC0 Spell night manager                                      | Kept the original Incident Report 77-B. The campaign's first informant.                                                                       |
| **Veronique Salt**       | Vice President, Crown & Cleaver Holdings                       | Brilliant, ruthless, ambitious. Signs off on the S1 recall. Becomes the party's rival, then their coup partner (S15, S23). Might betray them. |
| **Marda Hobb**           | Owner of the Tankard & Tallow; leader of the Last Independents | Refuses Murder King in S8. Her tavern becomes the party's war room.                                                                           |
| **Mortimer Pell**        | The murdered Murder King mascot performer                      | Walks after closing (S2). Knows who killed him and why. Remembers the original King.                                                          |
| **Counsel Ixabeth Vorr** | Erinyes attorney, Nine Circles Hospitality                     | Hell resents being acquired. She switches sides after S13 and files the motion for audit (S20).                                               |
| **Team Deliverables**    | Rival contractor party from GigGoblin                          | Take credit, steal jobs, level alongside the party. Their arc ends with them either signing for Creation & Company or not (see Bestiary).     |
| **Tallis Vend**          | Chairman, Creation & Company                                   | Unseen until S20. Polite, grey and certain. Owns himself.                                                                                     |

Chapter 4

## The Spine Missions

*Twenty-four missions carry the campaign from a burrito to the Signing Room. Each follows the same format: a corporate memo to read aloud, a summary, the hook, the DM's background, five scenes, the opposition, a twist, a Receipt, rewards and the thread that leads onward.*

Act I

## Employee of the Month

Levels 1–4

*Missing mascots, gelato oozes and striking unseen servants. The humour dominates—but the C&C sigil is stamped on every crate.*

**S1**

### The Wild Magic Burrito

Copper Level 1 · Client: [THAC0 Spell](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#f-thac0)

**THAC0 SPELL INCIDENT REPORT 77-B**
Unscheduled lower-kitchen personnel reassignment. Promotional product (Wild Burrito, Limited Time) generating off-menu outcomes in 1 of 6 customers. Contractor to identify responsible ingredient. *Refunds are not authorised at this time.*

*The party signs with QuestRabbit, takes their first job at the THAC0 Spell flagship, and finds the incident report that will end the campaign.*

Hook

Pip Ashworth, a harengon contractor liaison with a clipboard and boundless enthusiasm, hands the party a Contractor Agreement to sign—in quadruplicate—then a job slip that is still warm from the job board.

Background — DM Only

#### Scenes

1. **Onboarding at the Warren.** Pip reads the Contractor Agreement aloud, skipping the parts he's been told not to read. Anyone who studies it (Investigation DC 20, or detect magic) sees Clause 77-B shimmer: ‘Signatory is appointed Representative of the Commons for purposes of future transactions.’ Pip shrugs: ‘Legal wrote that. They're very thorough!’ The party receives badges and a one-star Rabbit Rating.
2. **Lunch Rush.** The flagship is packed with guards, wizards and students. Each round, roll a d6 per burrito eater; on a 1, roll on the wild magic surge table. The party must keep order while tasting the menu for clues. A goblin customer has turned blue; a guard is floating; a wizard is now two wizards and they're arguing.
3. **The Ingredient.** Kitchen investigation: the only new ingredient is a jar of red powder labelled ‘Promotional Seasoning—C&C Supply.’ It hums. Detect magic reveals wild, old magic—older than the building. Hollis Quench, twitchy and sleepless, pulls the party aside and asks them to look at the cellar.
4. **The Cellar with Two Doors.** One door leads to storage. The other is iron, older than the foundations, and warm to the touch. Behind it, faintly, a register rings. Hollis gives the party the original Incident Report 77-B: Dobbs's disappearance, the date, and the words he shouted back—‘It's still open! They're still serving!’ The door will not open for them yet (it opens in S14).
5. **Corporate Resolution.** Head Office sends a courier with a sealed memo: ‘Promotion concluded. Seasoning recalled. Contractor thanked.’ The courier asks for the report. Whether the party hands over the forgery or the original is their choice—and the first real decision of the campaign.

Opposition

A wild-magic flumph-like spice spirit that forms from the jar if it's opened (use a dust mephit with random elemental damage), 4 surging customers (commoners with random effects), 1 animated tortilla press (animated armor).

Twist

Team Deliverables, a rival contractor party from GigGoblin, arrives halfway through to ‘consult’ and tries to take credit.

Rewards

50 gp less 30% (35 gp). One star added to the Rabbit Rating. A THAC0 Spell staff discount.

Forward

Veronique Salt, Crown & Cleaver's VP, was the one who signed off on the recall. The C&C sigil appears again in every mission of Act I. Hollis becomes an informant.

Receipt

**Incident Report 77-B (original)**

Hollis's handwritten report, with a line cook's last words and the C&C supply stamp. It shares a number with the Contractor Agreement's Clause 77-B, which no one notices yet.

Voids Clause 77-B — Signature Authority

**S2**

### The King Is Dead

Copper Level 1–2 · Client: [Murder King](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#f-murderking)

**MURDER KING BRAND CONTINUITY NOTICE**
Mascot performer M. Pell has experienced a permanent end-of-shift. The mascot continues after-hours community engagement. Contractor to facilitate ‘brand quietude’ before the Coronation Day promotion.

*A murdered mascot performer and a costume that keeps walking. The party meets Veronique Salt and discovers that brands can be alive.*

Hook

Pip calls it ‘a great second job, very visible!’ Murder King is offering a bonus if it's done before Coronation Day.

Background — DM Only

#### Scenes

1. **The Grand Throne Restaurant.** Shift lead Duckworth Pym explains: Mortimer died in the costume, the costume was put in storage, and now it isn't. Veronique Salt arrives in a black carriage, charming and chilly, and tells the party she wants the costume ‘retired with dignity’—and anything found inside it returned to her, unread.
2. **Night Sightings.** A night-hunt across Coronation Square. The costume appears at children's parties that aren't happening, waves at empty streets, and stands outside the newspaper office. Witnesses say it was ‘looking for something.’
3. **Mortimer's Flat.** Mortimer's landlady lets the party in. His notebook contains a copy of the trademark deed (in his handwriting) and notes: ‘The King's not a mascot. He was real. Somebody killed him and bought the murder.’ There's also an appointment with a reporter at the Brandmere Crier, tonight.
4. **The Newspaper Office.** The costume attacks the reporter at midnight. It fights like a king—proud, cruel, theatrical. Destroying it is hard; convincing it that Mortimer's death harms the brand (Persuasion DC 14 with the notebook as evidence) makes it collapse like empty cloth.
5. **Veronique's Offer.** Veronique asks for the notebook. If the party gives it, she thanks them and burns it before their eyes (they may have copied it). If they refuse, she smiles and says, ‘Then keep it safe. You'll find it's worth more to you than to me.’ She doesn't mean it kindly.

Opposition

The Murder King Costume (use a wight with the brand egregore trait: regains 5 hp whenever someone within 30 ft says ‘Murder King’), 3 Crown & Cleaver cleaners (thugs) who arrive to collect the notebook.

Twist

The costume can be talked to. It believes it is the King. It does not know it is only advertising.

Rewards

100 gp less 30%. One star if the costume is stopped without the newspaper reporter dying.

Forward

Veronique becomes the recurring face of corporate opposition. The deed names Baron Cleave, setting up S15. The idea that brands have living egregores returns with Krispy Kobold's HOT sign and McGuffin's toys.

Receipt

**Mortimer Pell's Notebook**

A copy of the four-hundred-year-old deed and a mascot performer's notes on the real King Aldric. Proof that Creation & Company concealed how it acquired its first kingdom.

Voids Clause 11 — Disclosure

**S3**

### Gelli Goes Missing

Silver Level 2 · Client: [Gelatinous Cube](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#f-gelcube)

**GELATINOUS CUBE CUSTOMER RETENTION EVENT**
Three team members currently remain within company property outside scheduled working hours. Brand mascot (Gelli) presently unaccounted for. Discretion essential. Retention figures remain excellent.

*Missing workers, a missing mascot, and a gelatinous cube who is loyal to the wrong people. The party learns that the Sweet Covenant ‘retains’ employees literally.*

Hook

A frantic call from Bettina Soft, the Canal Street Creamery manager, who insists everything is fine, very fine, extremely fine.

Background — DM Only

#### Scenes

1. **The Creamery.** Bettina's staff are too quiet. The walk-in freezer hums a lullaby. The staff lockers of the missing three contain their employment contracts, with the Retention Clause circled in red ink by one of them.
2. **The Canals.** Tracking a transparent cube through canals at night: a Perception challenge with slime trails, lost boots and a boatman who saw ‘a big wobbly box’ floating toward the Sweet Covenant's warehouse.
3. **The Sweet Covenant Warehouse.** Covenant handlers (enchanted by Granny Quean) have found Gelli and are loading it onto a barge bound for the Deep Dairy. The party must stop the barge.
4. **Saving the Retained.** Freeing the workers without killing them: feed Gelli something tastier (Animal Handling DC 13), or fight it carefully. Each round, each worker inside takes acid damage. Once freed, they testify about the Retention Clause.
5. **Corporate Resolution.** Bettina asks for Gelli back. The workers want the contracts destroyed. The party can return Gelli to Bettina or free it—it will follow whoever fed it.

Opposition

Gelli (gelatinous cube, 84 hp, friendly if fed), 4 Covenant handlers (bandits), 1 green hag supervisor, Auntie Curdle (if not met before).

Twist

Gelli likes the party. A domesticated gelatinous cube is a surprisingly useful companion.

Rewards

150 gp less 30%. One star. Possibly a pet gelatinous cube.

Forward

The freed workers mention the Deep Dairy and ‘the Mother down below’. This sets up S18.

Receipt

**The Retention Clauses**

Three signed employment contracts with the Retention Clause: evidence that the Sweet Covenant enforces contracts by consuming its workers.

— (Leverage +1 vs Sweet Covenant; contributes to S18)

**S4**

### Union of Unseen Servants

Silver Level 3 · Client: [Starkfucks](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#f-starkfucks)

**STARKFUCKS PARTNER ENGAGEMENT MEMO**
Invisible workforce engaging in unapproved collective inactivity. Unseen servants cannot legally unionise. Contractor to restore throughput before morning rush.

*Forty invisible workers go on strike at the Reserve Roastery. The party learns they were once customers who sold their presence.*

Hook

Saffron Mellow, the roastery manager, calls in QuestRabbit because nobody can see who they're negotiating with.

Background — DM Only

#### Scenes

1. **The Silent Roastery.** Cups float, stop, and lower. The machines are off. Chalk writing on the menu board reads ‘WE ARE PEOPLE’. Saffron insists it's a spell malfunction.
2. **Meeting the Steward.** Agnes speaks through a floating pen. Her demands: their bodies back, back pay, and acknowledgement. She shows the party their loyalty contracts, stored in the back office, each signed while ‘enjoying a complimentary Reserve blend’.
3. **Management's Response.** ABI sends a union-breaking wizard, who plans to dispel them all. The party must choose: protect the strikers, or let the wizard proceed. Dispelling them kills them.
4. **The Back Office.** The original contracts are locked in a warded filing cabinet. The contracts show that the ‘presence’ was sold to Arcane Beverage International and that a copy was sent to the Counting House.
5. **Negotiated Settlement.** If the party wins the strike, the servants regain partial visibility (they appear as faint outlines). They owe the party a favour—and forty invisible allies is a lot of favour.

Opposition

ABI union-breaker (mage), 4 Starkfucks security (guards), 1 animated espresso machine (helmed horror, reduced), and the enchanted grinder if not dealt with in The Grind.

Twist

If the party drinks the Reserve blend, they feel very agreeable—DC 13 Wisdom save or agree with the next suggestion Saffron makes.

Rewards

250 gp less 30%. One star if the strike ends without deaths. Forty invisible friends.

Forward

Agnes's union spreads. The first mention of Compliance and Pandemonium, which pays off in S11.

Receipt

**The Loyalty Contracts**

Forty signed contracts transferring presence in exchange for coffee, each signed under Compliance. Proof that consent in Creation & Company contracts is coerced.

Voids Clause 3 — Free Consent

**S5**

### The Crown Jewels

Silver Level 4 · Client: [Murder King](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#f-murderking)

**MURDER KING ASSET RECOVERY DIRECTIVE**
Enchanted promotional crown (Coronation Day edition) relocated without authorisation to a competitor's premises. Contractor to recover. Competitor relationship to remain cordial.

*A heist into Wight Castle to recover a crown that really does make kings, ending with proof that Murder King and Wight Castle share an owner.*

Hook

Veronique Salt hires the party personally, as ‘a thank-you for your discretion.’ She pays upfront.

Background — DM Only

#### Scenes

1. **Casing the Castle.** Wight Castle's Old Gate branch by day: pale staff, too-bright lights, the Coffin Box on the menu. Mortimer is behind the counter and knows exactly why the party is here.
2. **The Graveyard Shift.** A night infiltration. After midnight the staff become ghosts reconciling receipts. The party can sneak, fight, or ask Mortimer for help (he'll help if they promise to vouch for him in a future review).
3. **The Vault.** A small undead vault beneath the kitchen, with the crown on a velvet pillow. The toy key from The Missing McGuffin opens it without a check; otherwise DC 16 Thieves' Tools. The vault's crest is the C&C sigil—the same on Murder King's crates.
4. **Long Live the Party.** Anyone who puts on the crown becomes the rightful monarch of the ground beneath their feet: a street, a room, a vault. Loyal subjects appear: the nearby rats, then the zombies. It's fun, then worrying.
5. **Veronique's Proposal.** Veronique receives the crown, examines the party, and offers them an exclusive retainer with Crown & Cleaver. She also says something she will regret: ‘Don't be so shocked. C&C owns both. It owns everything, eventually. And not the C&C you think.’

Opposition

4 specter night staff, 4 zombie cooks, 1 wight (vault guard), Mortimer (only if betrayed; use a wight with a perfect memory).

Twist

Team Deliverables are also in the vault, hired by McGuffin's to steal the crown for the Chosen Customer promotion.

Rewards

400 gp less 30%. Two stars total at the end of Act I (the Rabbit Rating should be at two stars). Mortimer's friendship.

Forward

End of Act I. The party is offered a Crown & Cleaver retainer, sees the C&C sigil everywhere, and hears Veronique's slip: ‘not the C&C you think’.

Receipt

**The Vault Inventory**

Wight Castle's vault manifest, listing the crown as ‘held for C&C Holdings on behalf of parent’. Proof that apparent rivals share an owner.

— (Leverage +1 vs Crown & Cleaver; key clue)

Act II

## The Franchise Wars

Levels 5–8

*Corporations sabotage one another openly. The party is offered competing contracts and learns that ledgers can rewrite reality.*

**S6**

### The Missing Spice

Silver Level 5 · Client: [THAC0 Spell](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#f-thac0)

**THAC0 SPELL SUPPLY CONTINUITY ALERT**
Proprietary Seventh Secret Spice subject to unauthorised relocation. Evidence indicates competitor involvement (Critical Wok). Recovery priority: high. Violence: at contractor's discretion.

*A spice war between THAC0 Spell and Critical Wok reveals both belong to Probability Brands Group and the spice is a Pandemonium extract.*

Hook

Hollis Quench asks for the party by name. The flagship's customers have become restless and unpleasant since the spice vanished—withdrawal.

Background — DM Only

#### Scenes

1. **Withdrawal.** THAC0 Spell's regulars are twitchy, angry, and fixated. The party can calm a riot while tracing the theft: the spice jar was taken by a Critical Wok delivery driver.
2. **Critical Wok.** A competitive noodle bar where every customer rolls a d20 to order. Lucky Oyelaran rolls high every time. The spice is in the back, being cooked into ‘Seventh Heaven Noodles’. The queues are enormous.
3. **The PBG Lab.** Behind Critical Wok is a Probability Brands Group lab testing customer behaviour: charts of queue length, luck extraction readings, and a memo from Deuce Marchetti.
4. **The Choice.** Returning the spice makes THAC0 Spell's customers calm again. Destroying it causes withdrawal, but frees them. A third choice: give it to Petronella Fizzwick (Ale & Error), who can make an antidote.

Opposition

PBG lab staff (2 cult fanatics, 1 mage), Lucky Oyelaran (bandit captain, advantage on every roll until his dice are taken), 2 gladiator ‘chefs’.

Twist

The memo is addressed to both THAC0 Spell's regional manager and Critical Wok's. The ‘rivals’ run the same experiment.

Rewards

600 gp less 30%.

Forward

The spice trail leads to Pandemonium and the Compliance plantations (S11).

Receipt

**The Marchetti Memo**

A memo proving the spice war is staged, both restaurants belong to PBG, and the spice is from Pandemonium.

— (Leverage +1 vs PBG; sets up S11)

**S7**

### Executive Plot Device

Silver Level 5–6 · Client: [Big McGuffin's](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#f-bigmcguffins)

**BIG McGUFFIN'S SECURE LOGISTICS REQUEST**
Transport one (1) briefcase from Midnight Plotline to the listed delivery address. Opening the briefcase constitutes a termination event. Contractor is advised that interested parties may express interest.

*A briefcase must cross the city. Every faction wants it. Inside is the Quill of Consideration, the pen that signs the Grand Ledger.*

Hook

Maxim Knell hands over a locked black case, murmuring, ‘And so the heroes take the burden…’

Background — DM Only

#### Scenes

1. **Pick-Up.** At the Midnight Plotline the jukebox plays ominous strings. Three other parties watch the case being handed over.
2. **The Chase.** A city-wide chase: Crown & Cleaver cleaners on the rooftops, PHG invisible stalkers in the alleys, a Hoard kobold ambush in a sewer, Team Deliverables in a hired carriage. Run as a chase with complications.
3. **The Temptation.** The briefcase whispers. Anyone who opens it sees a golden quill and hears a voice: ‘Write something. Anything. It will be true.’ The quill can write one line in any document that becomes binding. Opening the case triggers a Compliance Officer alarm.
4. **The Delivery.** The delivery address is THAC0 Spell's cellar. The iron door is ajar. A hand in a grey sleeve reaches out, takes the case, and closes the door. A receipt is printed: ‘Received: Consideration. From: Representatives of the Commons.’

Opposition

Waves from four factions: 4 thugs, 2 invisible stalkers (weakened), 6 kobolds, 1 Team Deliverables. A Compliance Officer if the case is opened.

Twist

If the party opened the case and used the quill, the line they wrote is permanently true. Record it.

Rewards

750 gp less 30%. One star.

Forward

The Quill of Consideration returns in S24: whoever holds it signs the Final Contract. The receipt calls the party ‘Representatives of the Commons’—the same phrase as Clause 77-B.

Receipt

**Delivery Receipt**

A receipt from the Franchise Below, thanking the ‘Representatives of the Commons’ for the delivery. The party's names are on it.

— (Leverage +1 vs Creation & Company; key clue)

**S8**

### Hostile Takeover

Silver Level 6 · Client: [Murder King](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#f-murderking)

**CROWN & CLEAVER FRANCHISE EXPANSION BRIEF**
Contractor to facilitate franchise agreements with fourteen (14) independent taverns in the Old Independents' Quarter. Violence is neither encouraged nor explicitly prohibited.

*Veronique wants fourteen taverns signed. Taverns that sign vanish. The party meets Marda Hobb and the Last Independents.*

Hook

Veronique activates the party's Crown & Cleaver retainer (if they took it) or offers triple pay through QuestRabbit.

Background — DM Only

#### Scenes

1. **The List.** Fourteen taverns, each with a proprietor, a history, and a reason to refuse. Run as a sandbox; each tavern is a scene of charm, threat or bribery.
2. **The Vanished.** The party visits one of the three taverns that signed. It is now a Murder King. Nobody remembers it. But its old sign is in the bin out back, and the old owner is wandering the street asking where she lives.
3. **Marda Hobb.** Marda invites the party to the Tankard & Tallow. She has no contract to offer: only a handshake and the truth about what signing does.
4. **The Choice.** The party can sign the taverns, refuse, or sabotage the paperwork. Each unsigned deed kept safe counts. Veronique will retaliate: Little Caesars Legion arrives to ‘deliver’.
5. **The Siege of Tallow Lane.** If the party sides with Marda, the Legion attacks the Quarter. A street battle with barricades, kegs and fire.

Opposition

Little Caesars Legion: 8 legionnaires (veterans), 1 centurion (gladiator), Veronique Salt (see Bestiary) if the party is openly defiant.

Twist

Veronique doesn't want to win this. She wants to see what the party does. If they defy her, she offers them a secret deal later (Succession Planning).

Rewards

1,000 gp less 30% if the taverns sign. A place at Marda's table if they refuse. One star either way (QuestRabbit counts completion, not outcome—note this).

Forward

The Last Independents become the resistance. Their handshake network is invisible to the Ledger. Commons Tally begins.

Receipt

**The Unsigned Deeds**

The original deeds of every tavern that refused, kept by Marda Hobb. Proof that the Material Plane's ‘subsidiaries’ were never wholly acquired.

Voids Clause 5 — Title to Subsidiaries

**S9**

### Where's Number Four?

Silver Level 7 · Client: [Five Wights](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#f-fivewights)

**FIVE WIGHTS PARTNER AVAILABILITY CONCERN**
Partner No. 4 unavailable at all locations. Operational reality impacted: menu items reported as ‘existing slightly less’. Contractor to locate partner.

*One of the Five Wights is missing, and reality is fraying. He is being held as collateral by Creation & Company in the CostCo-op executive vault.*

Hook

Brothers One, Two, Three and Five ask for the party at every Five Wights at once.

Background — DM Only

#### Scenes

1. **Reality Glitches.** At every Five Wights, the fries flicker, the customers repeat sentences, and the brothers finish each other's sentences with Four's words. The oath is failing.
2. **Following the Oath.** The brothers can feel where Four is: north, cold, stacked. They lead the party to the CostCo-op warehouse.
3. **The Warehouse.** A membership card is needed to enter (Membership Drive's cards, a forgery, or a real sign-up—which binds descendants). Inside: aisles miles long, free samples of the Mother Culture, and staff in vests.
4. **The Executive Vault.** A vault guarded by dragons' kobolds and a Compliance Officer. Four is in a box, dormant. Opening the box without the Ledger's permission sounds an alarm.
5. **Collateral Recovery.** Carrying Four out, with the vault waking up behind the party.

Opposition

1 Compliance Officer (see Bestiary), 6 kobolds, 2 guard drakes, 4 mimic pallets.

Twist

The vault holds other collateral: a king's soul, a city's river, a whole year. The party can take one more item—but the Ledger will notice.

Rewards

700 gp less 30%. The Five Wights' eternal gratitude (a free meal at any branch forever).

Forward

The CostCo-op vault and its seven dragon directors return in S19. Four is a witness in S24.

Receipt

**Four's Collateral Tag**

The tag from Four's box, proving Creation & Company holds sentient beings as collateral. Four himself becomes a witness.

Voids Clause 8 — Collateral

**S10**

### Always Low Prices

Silver Level 8 · Client: [Wand-Mart](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#f-wandmart)

**ADVENTURE RETAIL CONSORTIUM INTERNAL AUDIT**
Cost structure anomalies identified in discount inventory. Contractor to investigate on behalf of an anonymous shareholder.

*The party discovers how Wand-Mart sells below cost: customers pay in years, debited through the Grand Ledger. They find their first Folio.*

Hook

The ‘anonymous shareholder’ is Veronique Salt. She wants leverage against ARC—and the Board.

Background — DM Only

#### Scenes

1. **Rollback Day.** A sale day at the Supercentre. Chaos, crowds, bargains. The party watches customers age a few days with each purchase.
2. **The Numbers.** Investigate the stockroom: invoices show items cost more than they sell for. Where's the difference? A clerk mentions ‘time payments’.
3. **The Cash Office.** A heist into the cash office. Doris Plank is there, unmoved, with the Folio on the desk. She explains, cheerfully, how it works—and offers the party lifetime free shopping.
4. **The Folio.** The Folio's entries rewrite themselves as the party watches. Writing in it makes things true, but uses the writer's time. Veronique wants it.
5. **Veronique's Confession.** Veronique reveals the truth: ‘C&C doesn't stand for Crown & Cleaver. It stands for Creation & Company. We all belong to it. So do you.’ She offers the party a partnership against the Board.

Opposition

Doris Plank (see Bestiary, as Veronique's statblock reskinned), 4 shelf mimics, 2 helmed horrors, 1 SKÜ drone (giant mimic).

Twist

The Folio has a page for the party: ‘Representatives of the Commons—Rating: 3 stars—Mandate Progress: 60%.’

Rewards

1,200 gp less 30%. A Folio (see Magic Items).

Forward

End of Act II. The party learns C&C means Creation & Company and sees a Mandate Progress meter beside their Rabbit Rating.

Receipt

**The Wand-Mart Cost Ledger**

Pages of the Folio showing years debited from customers. Proof that Creation & Company's purchases are not made at fair value.

Voids Clause 2 — Consideration

Act III

## Hostile Takeover

Levels 9–12

*The corporations buy concepts: the word healing, the laws of Hell, a founder's immortality. The party opens the door beneath THAC0 Spell.*

**S11**

### The Beans of Pandemonium

Gold Level 9 · Client: [Starkfucks](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#f-starkfucks)

**ARCANE BEVERAGE INTERNATIONAL PROCUREMENT ORDER**
Seasonal premium crop procurement on an adjacent plane. Standard hazard pay applies. Contractors are advised to wear ear protection.

*A procurement run to Pandemonium's howling plantations reveals Compliance, the bean that makes the world agreeable, and the lost people who harvest it.*

Hook

ABI, unaware the party has turned (or hoping they haven't), hires them for the harvest. Pip calls it ‘a big step up!’

Background — DM Only

#### Scenes

1. **The Portal.** A PHG-run portal from Bean There, Summoned That. The wind begins before the party arrives.
2. **The Plantations.** Endless tunnels of glowing beans. The harvesters hum. Each Wisdom save failed makes the party forget one thing (players choose).
3. **The Harvesters.** Harvesters who remember nothing but their shifts. One, a woman called Ada, remembers her daughter's name and nothing else. She is Agnes Fairweather's sister (S4).
4. **The Overseer.** A cambion overseer on a howler. Beating him opens the processing shed, where Compliance is packed for shipment to every ABI branch.
5. **Sabotage or Harvest.** The party can bring back the beans as contracted, poison the crop, or free the harvesters and lead them home.

Opposition

Cambion overseer (cambion), 4 howlers, 1 wind-elemental storm (air elemental ×2), Pandemonium's madness (DC 14 Wisdom save each hour).

Twist

Burning the crop ends Compliance for the season; ABI will know the party did it.

Rewards

2,000 gp less 30% if harvested. The freed harvesters' gratitude if not.

Forward

ABI's supply is cut; Ulth'Arabica takes notice. Compliance starts wearing off across the city.

Receipt

**The Harvesters' Testimony**

Signed statements (with handshakes, not quills) from freed harvesters. Leverage against ABI; the harvesters join the Commons Tally.

— (Leverage +2 vs ABI)

**S12**

### All Rights Reserved

Gold Level 9–10 · Client: [Goodberry's](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#f-goodberrys)

**ARCANE BEVERAGE INTERNATIONAL WELLNESS ANNOUNCEMENT**
Effective immediately, the term HEALING™ is a registered trademark of ABI Wellness. Unlicensed use of HEALING™ in prayers, potions or spells will incur a licensing fee.

*ABI buys the word ‘healing’. Cure wounds spells fail without a licence. The party must find the original deed to Healing and set it free.*

Hook

Brother Allard (Death Save Delivery) sends word: clerics across the city can't heal. People are dying in the temples.

Background — DM Only

#### Scenes

1. **Temples in Crisis.** Clerics chanting, spells fizzling. A licensing booth outside each temple, selling HEALING™ by the prayer.
2. **The Shrine.** The shrine's keeper, Sister Wren, explains the deed. She was forced to sign away Healing to save her dying son.
3. **Goodberry's Head Office.** A heist through a wellness-themed office: meditation rooms, juice bars, legal department. The deed is in a vault of trademarks, alongside deeds to Sleep, Courage and Weekends.
4. **The Trademark Hearing.** ABI takes the party to a Guild Court trademark hearing. The party argues that Healing cannot be owned. Use a debate structure: three arguments, three Persuasion checks, each with evidence.
5. **Freeing Healing.** If the party wins or steals the deed, they can write ‘owned by nobody’ on it. Healing returns to the world.

Opposition

ABI legal team (2 rakshasas in lawyer suits, lowered to level 10), 4 knights, 1 Goodberry's druid.

Twist

The deeds to Sleep, Courage and Weekends are also in the vault. The party can free one more—or all, if they're brave.

Rewards

Clerics' gratitude. Brother Allard's temple becomes a Last Independents sanctuary.

Forward

Healing, the personified concept, remains in the Third Place until S17.

Receipt

**The Healing Deed**

The original deed to Healing, proving the concept was never lawfully owned by Creation & Company's subsidiaries.

Voids Clause 9 — Non-Infringement

**S13**

### Pact of the Bucket

Gold Level 10 · Client: [Chick'n Familiar](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#f-chicknfamiliar)

**CHICK'N FAMILIAR SUPPLIER INVOICE — MATURED**
Legacy supplier (infernal) has presented invoice for original franchise consideration. Contractor to facilitate settlement. Payment in souls is not authorised.

*A devil comes to collect on Chick'n Familiar's franchise contract, and Counsel Ixabeth Vorr reveals that Hell is being acquired by Creation & Company.*

Hook

Wendel Clasp begs for help: his franchise agreement's payment is due at midnight.

Background — DM Only

#### Scenes

1. **The Midnight Invoice.** A barbed devil collector arrives with a contract and a bucket.
2. **The Contract.** Examining the contract (Investigation DC 18) reveals a loophole: the payment is in ‘the first soul that enters the restaurant after midnight’. The party can send in a chicken.
3. **Counsel Ixabeth Vorr.** Ixabeth appears, amused, and proposes a deal: the party gets the loophole enforced if they deliver her due-diligence report to someone who can use it.
4. **The Due-Diligence Report.** The report explains that Creation & Company's claims on the Material Plane are not recognised under infernal law, and that Hell has a competing jurisdiction claim.
5. **The Offer.** Vellichor wants an alliance. ‘Even Hell has antitrust concerns.’ The party can accept, refuse or negotiate.

Opposition

1 barbed devil collector, 4 imps, 1 chain devil, Chickadee (a quasit, the mascot) as a sneaky wildcard.

Twist

If the party refuses Vellichor's alliance, he offers them a smaller one: his vote in S23 in exchange for the report reaching the Counting House.

Rewards

2,500 gp less 30%. Counsel Ixabeth Vorr's card.

Forward

Hell is now an uneasy ally. Ixabeth Vorr represents the party at the AGM (S20).

Receipt

**Infernal Due-Diligence Report**

An erinyes attorney's report proving Creation & Company's claim on the Material Plane is contested under infernal jurisdiction.

Voids Clause 10 — Governing Law

**S14**

### The Franchise Below

Gold Level 11 · Client: [THAC0 Spell](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#f-thac0)

**THAC0 SPELL HERITAGE ASSESSMENT**
Discovery of non-inventoried legacy premises beneath flagship location. Assess for heritage status and trading licence. Premises reportedly still trading.

*The sealed iron door beneath THAC0 Spell opens onto the first shop in history, still serving customers who never left. The party finds the Ledger's First Entry.*

Hook

Hollis Quench calls the party. The door is open. Dobbs, the missing line cook, is behind the counter.

Background — DM Only

#### Scenes

1. **The Door.** The iron door opens onto stairs that go down for too long.
2. **The First Shop.** A shop that's every shop: a counter, a scale, a till, a bell. Customers who have waited centuries, and Dobbs, now serving them, calm and blank.
3. **The Staff.** Ghostly clerks from every era. They are not hostile; they are busy. They will serve the party. Buying anything means signing.
4. **The First Entry.** Behind the counter is a lectern holding a page torn from the Grand Ledger's first volume: ‘Entry 1. Tallis Vend owns Tallis Vend, in perpetuity. Witnessed by: Tallis Vend.’
5. **The Door to the Counting House.** A second door behind the lectern: grey, locked, and labelled ‘Staff Only’. The party can't open it yet. They hear the scratch of a quill beyond.

Opposition

4 Compliance Officers (see Bestiary) guard the lectern if the page is taken. The Clerk of the First Sale (a ghost with lich stats, weakened).

Twist

Dobbs can be freed if the party pays for his ‘shift’ in a coin that means something to them.

Rewards

3,000 gp less 30%. Dobbs's gratitude.

Forward

The party knows where the Counting House is. The Staff Only door opens in S20.

Receipt

**The First Entry**

A self-witnessed entry. Proof that Creation & Company's founding act was a circular claim that no one else witnessed.

Voids Clause 1 — Identity of Purchaser

**S15**

### Long Live the King

Platinum Level 12 · Client: [Murder King](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#f-murderking)

**CROWN & CLEAVER EMERGENCY BRAND PROTECTION**
Historic brand ambassador has returned and is asserting ownership claims. Legal and physical containment required. Budget unlimited.

*Aldric the Unkind, the real Murder King, returns to reclaim his kingdom and brand, and the party must decide what justice looks like.*

Hook

Veronique Salt calls in a panic: ‘He's in the Throne Tower. He's eating everyone.’

Background — DM Only

#### Scenes

1. **The Throne Tower.** A siege. Aldric is on the top floor, eating executives, wearing the promotional crown (from S5).
2. **The Revenant King.** Aldric is proud, cruel, and correct. He demands his kingdom and the death of Cleave.
3. **Baron Cleave.** Cleave arrives with death-knight guards. He intends to kill Aldric and anyone who witnessed the truth.
4. **The Choice.** The party can ally with Aldric against Cleave, stop both, or broker a deal: Aldric gets his revenge and relinquishes his claim.
5. **Aftermath.** If Cleave is destroyed, Crown & Cleaver loses its Board seat and Veronique becomes CEO.

Opposition

Aldric the Unkind (revenant with death knight stats), Baron Ossian Cleave (death knight), 6 wights, 2 helmed horrors.

Twist

Veronique wants Cleave dead so she can take his seat. She helps—until she doesn't.

Rewards

5,000 gp less 30%. Five stars possible here—if the Rabbit Rating reaches five, go straight to S16.

Forward

End of Act III. The Rabbit Rating is at four or five stars. Harriet Hopwell's warning (Performance Review) matters now.

Receipt

**Aldric's Testimony**

The first acquired king's testimony. Leverage against Crown & Cleaver and Creation & Company.

— (Leverage +2 vs Crown & Cleaver; Aldric can be a witness in S24)

Act IV

## Market Domination

Levels 13–16

*Pocket dimensions, primordial oozes and dragon boards. The party is invited to the Annual General Meeting and sees their own names on the Final Contract.*

**S16**

### Do Not Disturb

Platinum Level 13 · Client: [Long Rest Inn](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#f-longrest)

**LONG REST INN HOUSEKEEPING REQUEST**
Room 303 has displayed DO NOT DISTURB for ninety-four years. Corporate requests the room be cleaned and the occupants invited to check out.

*The Last Five-Star Party has been sleeping in Room 303 for ninety-four years, hiding from signing the Final Contract. They reveal what the Rabbit Rating really measures.*

Hook

Pip, nervous, hands over a Black Card-adjacent contract: ‘They said you're the only ones qualified.’

Background — DM Only

#### Scenes

1. **The Room.** A door with a sign, a century of dust, and a time-stop field. Getting in requires a counterspell, a wish, or Cadwyn's song (Authenticity Guaranteed).
2. **The Sleepers.** Four heroes asleep: Cadwyn Lark (bard), Tomas Brand (paladin), Ysolde Fenn (wizard), and Hark (ranger). Waking one at a time is possible.
3. **The Truth.** Cadwyn explains: the Rabbit Rating measures public trust. At five stars, the party can sign for the commons. Every good deed brought them closer.
4. **The Compliance Officers.** The Counting House knows the room has been opened. Compliance Officers arrive to escort the Last Five-Star Party to the signing. Or the party.
5. **The Choice.** The party can let the old heroes sleep, or wake them to fight. Either way, they now know what they are.

Opposition

4 Compliance Officers, 1 Senior Auditor (marut), and the Long Rest Inn's staff (bearded devils).

Twist

The Last Five-Star Party can give up their own signature authority by passing it to the new party—or refusing to.

Rewards

None from QuestRabbit. The Last Five-Star Party's allegiance.

Forward

The party must now either lower their rating or find another way. Harriet Hopwell will help if persuaded.

Receipt

**The Five-Star Testimony**

Testimony from the Last Five-Star Party that the Rabbit Rating is a mandate manufactured by QuestRabbit, not freely given by the public.

Voids Clause 4 — Capacity of Signatories

**S17**

### The Third Place

Platinum Level 14 · Client: [Starkfucks](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#f-starkfucks)

**STARKFUCKS REAL ESTATE EXPANSION BRIEF**
New branch construction in pocket dimensions encountering pre-existing tenancy. Contractor to clear the site. Tenants are not considered stakeholders, as they are not considered.

*Starkfucks is building in the Third Place, the grey limbo where Creation & Company stores everything it has acquired and forgotten. The party finds the exiled concepts—Healing, Weekends, Courage—and a Folio that lists them all.*

Hook

ABI, reeling from S11 and S12, still needs contractors; it hires the party through QuestRabbit on autopilot. Alternatively, Healing itself (if met in The Hole Problem) leaves a donut hole on the party's pillow with a note: ‘Help.’

Background — DM Only

#### Scenes

1. **The Way In.** Entrances: the IKEA Mimic wardrobe (Assembly Required), the Dungeon Donuts storeroom crack, or a new Starkfucks door marked ‘Branch 1 of ∞’. The first step inside drains colour; each hour, every creature makes a DC 15 Wisdom save or forgets one proper noun until they leave (players choose which).
2. **The Grey Café.** A café that goes on forever. Tables of lost things, arranged by acquisition date. The party can find items from their own past (a toy, a letter)—the Ledger took them when they signed their first QuestRabbit agreement, as ‘onboarding consideration’. Retrieving one's own item grants inspiration.
3. **The Concepts.** Healing (thin, kind, grateful if freed in S12, weak if not), Weekends (exhausted), Courage (hiding, ironically), Sleep (dozing), and Rest (sold by Nine Circles). They explain their situation in soft voices. Courage asks the party to lend it some; any creature that does gains advantage on saves against being frightened for the rest of the campaign, and the concept regains its strength.
4. **The Construction Site.** ABI's crews are building a Starkfucks around a pillar made of concepts. The foreman is a thrall of Ulth'Arabica, and the aboleth can speak through him. Stone golems lay bricks of compressed grey. Stopping the build is a combat on scaffolding over nothing.
5. **The Site Office Folio.** The site office holds a Folio listing every concept, place and person in the Third Place. Writing ‘owned by nobody’ against a name frees it (each line costs the writer 1 hit point per level of the thing freed—a concept counts as level 10). The party must choose what to free with the time and blood they have.

Opposition

Thrall foreman (use a knight, with Ulth'Arabica able to cast dominate person through him once), 4 stone golems (construction crew), 2 grey concept-eaters (nightwalker-lite: use a wraith with 150 hp and the Annihilating Aura trait at 1d6).

Twist

Healing, if freed in S12, has been sheltering the others and knows a hidden exit that drops the party into Plane Inn's Room 13—where the Sleeper (Late Checkout) waits.

Rewards

No fee (ABI will not pay after this). The concepts' blessings: each freed concept grants the party one boon usable in S24 (Healing: every creature regains 50 hp; Courage: immunity to fear; Sleep: one Compliance Officer falls asleep; Weekends: an extra round in the clause debate).

Forward

Ulth'Arabica has seen the party through its thrall. ABI's director will be waiting in The Reservoir. The Board now considers the party a threat, not an asset—but Tallis Vend still needs their signatures.

Receipt

**The Third Place Folio**

A branch ledger listing every concept, place and life Creation & Company owns but does not use. Proof that the Company acquires far beyond any fair purpose. Freed concepts join the Commons Tally.

— (Leverage +2 vs ABI; each freed concept +1 Commons Tally)

**S18**

### The Mother Culture

Platinum Level 14–15 · Client: [Gelatinous Cube](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#f-gelcube)

**SWEET COVENANT SUPPLY CONTINUITY ALERT**
Legacy supplier awakening event in progress. Continuity of dessert supply is the priority. All other outcomes, including survival, are secondary.

*The primordial ooze beneath the Sweet Covenant's vats wakes up and calls every dessert home. The party descends into the Deep Dairy, confronts Granny Quean, and decides the fate of Sample Zero—proof that nothing in the Company's portfolio is perpetual.*

Hook

Bettina Soft calls in hysterics: every cube in the Canal Street Creamery is humming the freezer's lullaby, and the gelato is crawling out of the display.

Background — DM Only

#### Scenes

1. **The Crawling City.** Every Sweet Covenant shop is leaking. Gelato oozes toward the canals; donuts roll; blizzards crawl. The city watch (glazed by Dungeon Donuts) are trying to stop it with swords. The party can herd the desserts into the canals—they know the way home.
2. **The Glamoured Farm.** The Dairy Quean farm's glamour is failing; the farmhouse flickers into a sinkhole. Auntie Curdle guards the way down, bargaining desperately: she'll open the path in exchange for protection from Granny.
3. **The Deep Dairy.** A cavern of vats the size of lakes, filled with living cream that shows faces. Workers from S3's Retention Clause are here, alive, stirring; each freed worker is +1 Commons Tally.
4. **Granny Quean.** Granny is at the heart of the dairy, trying to force Sample Zero back into the Mother to re-establish control. She is charming, terrifying, and will offer each party member one day of their youth back per year they serve her.
5. **The Mother.** The Mother Culture rises: an ocean of ooze with a slow, deep voice that speaks through every dessert in the city. She will listen. Returning Sample Zero makes her whole and calm; she withdraws beneath the earth, taking her children, and the Sweet Covenant's product lines collapse overnight. Destroying her is possible but ends every Covenant shop—and the party loses her testimony.

Opposition

Granny Quean (night hag, 150 hp, casts as a 15th-level warlock: hold monster, dominate person, banishment), Auntie Curdle (green hag, if she sides with Granny), 4 ochre jellies, 2 black puddings, the Mother Culture (see Bestiary; she should not be fought if avoidable).

Twist

The Mother offers the party a gift: one drop of Sample Zero, which dissolves any written contract it touches. It can be used once in S24 to void any single clause without its Receipt.

Rewards

Bettina Soft's grateful testimony; the freed workers; the Mother's drop (see twist). The Sweet Covenant's Board vote is removed if Granny is defeated or deposed (see Coven Politics).

Forward

With ABI wounded and the Sweet Covenant broken, the Board convenes emergency sessions. Hal Grundy of CostCo-op calls QuestRabbit: the dragons must meet in person.

Receipt

**Sample Zero**

The first starter taken from the Mother Culture. Proof that every product in Creation & Company's portfolio began as something the Company never owned—and that nothing it holds is perpetual.

Voids Clause 12 — Perpetuity

**S19**

### Executive Membership

Platinum Level 15 · Client: [CostCo-op](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#f-costcoop)

**CostCo-op MEMBERSHIP SERVICES REQUEST**
Arrange in-person attendance of the executive board at an extraordinary meeting. Seven attendees. All are ancient dragons. Refreshments to be provided. Please do not be eaten.

*Seven ancient dragons have never attended a CostCo-op board meeting. The party must invite them, host them, and turn their vote—with the Twelfth Spice, with Receipts, or with nerve.*

Hook

Membership Manager Hal Grundy, sweating, explains that the Counting House requires a quorum of the Hoard Syndicate's owners to ratify its vote in the Final Acquisition. If the dragons don't show, the Hoard's vote defaults to Tallis Vend. If they do, the party gets a chance to speak.

Background — DM Only

#### Scenes

1. **Seven Invitations.** Deliver seven invitations, each a short scene: a riddle with Aurum, a bluff with the Twins, a gift for Frostmother Ysk, a legal notice for Old Tar. Grandmother Slate insists on seeing the party's Rabbit Rating—she respects stars.
2. **Catering.** The meeting is in the CostCo-op warehouse, cleared of aisles. The party runs catering: the Entire Roast Owlbear (or not, if they freed the owlbears), the Potion Barrel, and—if they have it—dragonroot hidden in the gravy. Each dose of dragonroot binds one dragon to whatever it says during the meeting.
3. **The Meeting.** Robert's Rules of Order among apex predators. Auntie Vermillia chairs. Every dragon has a grievance: Old Tar resents Vermillia; Aurum hates the whole business; Frostmother Ysk wants her youngest back from the Hoots & Owlbears farm.
4. **The Motion.** The party moves that the Hoard Syndicate withdraw from the Final Acquisition. Debate is a skill challenge: each Receipt and Leverage point against the Hoard grants advantage on one check. Kessik's royalty debt (Finger-Lichin' Good) and the Five Colours receipts are especially potent.
5. **The Vote.** Four votes carry it. Bound dragons vote as they spoke. If Vermillia loses, she attacks; the others will not help her if the vote was fair.

Opposition

Auntie Vermillia (ancient red dragon) if the vote goes against her; kobold loyalists (20 kobolds, 4 kobold dragonshields). A Compliance Officer observer who records the result and may try to annul it.

Twist

Pyrrhax (The Crusty Dragon) turns up as his grandmother's proxy if she refuses to attend—and votes with the party if they helped him in Delivery Territory.

Rewards

The Hoard Syndicate's proxy in S23. Dragons who voted with the party will fly to the Golden Tusk anchors in S21.

Forward

End of the dragons' neutrality. Invitations to the Annual General Meeting arrive the next morning.

Receipt

**The Board Minutes**

Minutes of the first in-person CostCo-op board meeting, recording a vote to withdraw. Leverage against the Hoard; the dragons' support matters in S21.

— (Leverage +2 vs Hoard; Hoard proxy for S23)

**S20**

### The Annual General Meeting

Platinum Level 16 · Client: [Creation & Company](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#u-cco)

**CREATION & COMPANY — NOTICE OF ANNUAL GENERAL MEETING**
All Directors and Representatives of the Commons are required to attend. Agenda Item 7: Acquisition of the Material Plane (progress). Refreshments will be provided. Attendance is not optional; it has been pre-recorded.

*The Staff Only door opens. The party walks into the Counting House, sits at the Board's table, audits the Grand Ledger, and sees the Final Contract with their own names typed at the bottom.*

Hook

An embossed invitation arrives, addressed to each character by full name, with the Rabbit Rating printed beside it. The Franchise Below's grey door now has a handle.

Background — DM Only

#### Scenes

1. **Reception.** A receptionist who is the party's QuestRabbit liaison Pip Ashworth—except it isn't; it's a Pip from a Ledger-page, a copy made when Pip signed. The real Pip is at home, alive. This one is gentle and sad and gives directions.
2. **The Floors.** The lift stops at floors the party didn't press: Acquisitions (where their own completed QuestRabbit contracts are framed), Collateral (Number Four's empty box, if rescued), Human Resources (a room full of five-star ratings waiting to be filled). Each floor is a short scene of dread and dark comedy.
3. **The Boardroom.** Every director present or by proxy: Veronique (or Cleave), Ulth'Arabica in a tank, Papa Morvhal, SKÜ as a boardroom table that breathes, Lady Crit, Duke Vellichor, Zafirah, Auntie Vermillia (or Pyrrhax), Deus Maxwell, Granny Quean (or an empty chair), and the Mother Culture's chair, dripping or empty. Tallis Vend welcomes the party as ‘our Representatives’.
4. **The Audit.** Counsel Ixabeth Vorr (if allied) files a motion for audit under infernal law; Vend grants it, amused. The party may read the Grand Ledger for 1 minute each. Each reader learns one true secret (DM's choice—often a Receipt they missed, or where Tallis Vend's weakness lies: Entry 1 has only one witness). Harriet Hopwell, if persuaded in Performance Review, stands and revokes the party's ratings; Vend corrects the Ledger with a flick of the Quill.
5. **The Final Contract.** Item 7. The Final Contract is read aloud. Thirteen clauses. The signing date is the night the Golden Tusks are lit. The party's names are typed on the signature line. Vend asks, politely, whether they have any questions. Then the Compliance Officers escort them out, with a courtesy copy.

Opposition

If the party attacks in the Boardroom: 10 Compliance Officers, a Senior Auditor (marut), and every hostile director. They should not attack; make this clear through Ixabeth or Pip-copy.

Twist

Veronique Salt passes the party a note under the table: ‘Extraordinary General Meeting. I'll call it. You bring the votes.’

Rewards

Knowledge, dread and an exit. The Rabbit Rating (or Mandate) is at five stars no matter what, because the Ledger says so.

Forward

End of Act IV. Three things must happen before the signing: the notarial seal must break (S21), the Acceptance must not be delivered (S22), and the Board must turn (S23).

Receipt

**The Final Contract (Courtesy Copy)**

A copy of Instrument of Acquisition No. 1. The party now knows every clause they must void and every Receipt they need.

— (reveals all 13 clauses)

Act V

## The Final Acquisition

Levels 17–20

*Break the seal, stop the delivery, turn the Board, and walk into the Signing Room with every Receipt they have.*

**S21**

### The Golden Tusks

Black Card Level 17 · Client: [The Golden Orcs](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#f-goldenorcs)

**GOLDEN ORCS NATIONAL SIGNAGE OPERATIONS**
Coordinated illumination of all tusk signage across the continent at the stroke of midnight, Acquisition Eve. Contractors to ensure no tusk remains dark. This job technically never existed.

*The golden tusks outside every Golden Orcs form a continental notarial seal. On Acquisition Eve they will light, and whatever is signed in the Counting House will be notarised for all reality. The party must break the seal.*

Hook

Grukka Sweetfang, the Golden Orcs manager who is honestly great with kids, finds the party at the Tankard & Tallow. She has the survey maps. She's been noticing for years.

Background — DM Only

#### Scenes

1. **The War Room.** The Tankard & Tallow becomes a command centre. Marda Hobb, Grukka, the Codfather families, kobold tunnel-runners and anyone else the party has befriended. Tally allies: each ally group can hold, scout or distract one site. Run it like a heist planning scene, with the map and three hours of real time before midnight.
2. **The Brandmere Grand Tusks.** In the heart of the city. If the kobold tunnels were preserved (Tunnel Vision), the party emerges beneath the sign unseen. If not, they fight through Little Caesars Legion lines. The tusk is a golden iron golem when disturbed.
3. **The Summit Tusks.** A mountain branch in a blizzard (Granny Quean's, if the Blizzard wasn't stopped). Dragons who voted with the party in S19 can carry the party here in minutes; otherwise it's a teleport or a very long night.
4. **The Drowned Tusks.** Beneath the harbour. The Codfather families provide water breathing. Cornelius Gaff's ghost (Sleeping with the Fishes) guides them. Drowned accountants still clock in.
5. **Midnight.** At midnight, every surviving tusk lights. If two anchors are broken, the light gutters and the seal cracks across the sky like a torn page. If only one is broken, the seal holds but is flawed: the party gains advantage on Clause 6's debate check in S24 rather than voiding it outright.

Opposition

Per anchor: 1 Golden Tusk construct (iron golem, 250 hp, radiant slam), 2 Compliance Officers. Grand Tusks adds 12 Legion veterans and 2 centurions; Summit Tusks adds a young white dragon; Drowned Tusks adds 4 drowned ghasts and a kraken priest.

Twist

Pizza the Hutt's land (The Hutt Below) was meant to be a fourth, reserve anchor. If the party sided with Grubbo, the reserve doesn't exist and the seal cracks with one fewer anchor.

Rewards

None paid; this job never existed. The allies who fought beside the party count toward the Commons Tally.

Forward

With the seal broken, Tallis Vend changes tactics. At dawn, Dominoancy's diviners start screaming.

Receipt

**The Broken Seal**

A shard of golden tusk, still warm. Proof that the Final Contract cannot be notarised.

Voids Clause 6 — Notarisation

**S22**

### The Last Slice

Black Card Level 18 · Client: [Dominoancy](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#f-dominoancy)

**DOMINOANCY PRIORITY DELIVERY — CROSS-PLANAR**
One (1) order to be fulfilled within a strict delivery window. The order has been placed. Customer names withheld. The customer is always right.

*Every diviner in Dominoancy has foreseen that one pizza order will end the world. The order is the Final Acceptance of Offer—in the party's names. It is being delivered to the Counting House across four planes. They must stop it.*

Hook

Madame Olive Aspire bursts into the Tankard & Tallow with her hair on end. ‘It's you. You ordered it. You haven't yet, but you will have. You did.’

Background — DM Only

#### Scenes

1. **The Ticket.** The order prints at every Dominoancy at once. Burning a copy does nothing; the original is in Rook's bag. Olive can foresee the route: Brandmere, the Third Place, Pandemonium, Avernus, the Counting House.
2. **Leg One: The Third Place.** A chase through the grey café. Freed concepts from S17 can slow Rook (Weekends stalls her; Sleep makes her drowsy). The party must catch her before she crosses a threshold.
3. **Leg Two: Pandemonium.** The howling wind-tunnels, now half-harvested (S11). Freed harvesters shout directions. The wind carries away anything not held tight—including, possibly, the pizza.
4. **Leg Three: Avernus.** Duke Vellichor's territory. If allied, his devils form a roadblock. If not, they join the chase for their own reasons. Ixabeth Vorr can file an injunction against delivery (Persuasion DC 20, advantage with the Due-Diligence Report).
5. **The Last Door.** Rook arrives at the Counting House's loading bay. She is sobbing; the prophecy compels her. The party can take the pizza by force, persuade her to break fate (Rook is freed by any creature offering her a delivery she'd rather make), or eat it. An order consumed cannot be delivered. The Last Slice tastes like every meal the party ever shared.

Opposition

Rook (use an assassin with foresight permanently active), Counting House couriers (4 quicklings of the Brass Keys), a Senior Auditor (marut) at the loading bay, and each plane's hazards.

Twist

If the party eats the pizza, each character gains the benefit of heroes' feast. And a memory: the first meal they had together, at THAC0 Spell.

Rewards

Rook's freedom and gratitude; Olive Aspire's foresight (one reroll for each character in S24).

Forward

The contract can no longer be accepted in the party's name. Tallis Vend must now get them to sign in person. Veronique calls the Extraordinary General Meeting.

Receipt

**The Undelivered Order**

The order ticket, grease-stained and undelivered. Proof the party never accepted the offer.

Voids Clause 7 — Delivery

**S23**

### The Shareholder Revolt

Black Card Level 19 · Client: [Creation & Company](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#u-cco)

**CREATION & COMPANY — NOTICE OF EXTRAORDINARY GENERAL MEETING**
Called at the requisition of a shareholder bloc. Sole agenda item: Removal of the Chairman. Proxies must be lodged in person.

*Veronique Salt calls an extraordinary meeting to remove Tallis Vend as Chairman. The party gathers proxies from the directors they've turned, destroyed or bargained with, and walks back into the Boardroom to vote.*

Hook

Veronique's card: ‘Six votes. Bring them. I'll bring the room.’

Background — DM Only

#### Scenes

1. **Counting Votes.** Before the meeting, tally the proxies. Common sources: Papa Morvhal (phylactery or Papa's Back), Lady Crit (The Big Game or Loaded Dice), Duke Vellichor (S13 report or Closing Time), the Hoard (S19), Deus Maxwell (The Final Twist), Crown & Cleaver (Veronique, if Cleave is gone). Removed seats: ABI (The Reservoir), ARC (The Showroom), Sweet Covenant (S18), PHG (Portal Monopoly). Each missing vote can be chased this session as a short scene.
2. **The Last Holdout.** Choose the director the party has not dealt with. Run their umbrella's Platinum contract in compressed form (one strong scene and one fight) as the final push.
3. **The Meeting.** The Boardroom again. Proxies are lodged in person, each director stepping forward to hand the party a slip of paper or refusing. Leverage points spent here give advantage on persuading wavering directors. Commons Tally allies may attend as observers; they cannot vote but they can stare.
4. **The Vote.** Read the tally aloud. If six seats are gone from Vend, the motion carries. Tallis Vend is removed as Chairman. The Ledger records it. The Counting House shakes.
5. **‘I Own Myself’.** Vend smiles. ‘You may remove the Chairman. You cannot remove what owns itself.’ He walks to the signing room, and the directors, one by one, simply leave. Veronique, if she survived, takes Crown & Cleaver and walks out too, pausing only to say: ‘Void him.’

Opposition

Compliance Officers who try to annul proxies (1 per contested vote; defeating one validates the proxy). Veronique betrays the party if they have no Leverage against her—she tries to take the Chair herself; the party can stop her or let her try and fail.

Twist

If fewer than six seats turn, the motion fails—but every flipped director still grants the party one advantage die in S24.

Rewards

The Board is broken. Creation & Company is now a company with no board and one owner of himself.

Forward

The signing room is open. Tallis Vend waits with the Quill.

Receipt

**The Minutes of Removal**

Minutes recording Tallis Vend's removal as Chairman. He enters S24 without Legendary Resistance and without his directors' protection.

— (weakens Tallis Vend in S24)

**S24**

### Awaiting Signature

Black Card Level 20 · Client: [Creation & Company](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#u-cco)

**INSTRUMENT OF ACQUISITION No. 1**
PROPERTY BEING ACQUIRED: Material Plane and associated subsidiaries. PURCHASE PRICE: Undisclosed. CURRENT OWNER: Disputed. PROPOSED NEW OWNER: Creation & Company. STATUS: Awaiting Signature.

*The finale. In a small grey room at the heart of the Counting House, Tallis Vend lays the Final Contract before the party and hands them the Quill. They void it clause by clause with every Receipt they have gathered since level 1—and then decide what to sign, if anything.*

Hook

There is no hook. The door is open. There is a table, a contract, a quill and a small grey man who has been waiting four hundred years.

Background — DM Only

#### Scenes

1. **The Signing Room.** A small room. A desk. The Grand Ledger, bound in grey, open to a blank page. Tallis Vend sits opposite and offers tea. He explains, calmly, that the party has earned their stars; that the commons trust them; that he will be a very good owner—no war, no hunger, everything managed. He means it. He's persuasive: every character makes a DC 20 Wisdom save or believes him until the first clause is voided.
2. **The Clause Debate.** Run as structured combat. Each round, Vend reads a clause aloud. A character presents the matching Receipt and makes an argument (Persuasion, Insight, History or Religion—DC 18, advantage from Leverage, allied witnesses or relevant boons). Success voids the clause: it burns off the page. Failure lets Vend make a Ledger Entry (see his stat block). Clauses without Receipts need a DC 25 argument, or Sample Zero's drop, or a witness such as the Sleeper.
3. **The Chairman Fights.** When six clauses are voided, Vend stands. The room becomes the whole Counting House, every filing cabinet a weapon, every Compliance Officer a hand. Use Tallis Vend's stat block. He cannot be killed while Entry 1 stands; he can only be reduced to 0 and rewrite himself (once per clause remaining). Keep voiding clauses mid-combat—each void is an action, and each one hurts him.
4. **Entry 1.** When all clauses are void (or Vend is reduced to 0 with no clauses left), the Ledger falls open to Entry 1. The party presents the First Entry and says—any words will do—‘We witness you.’ Tallis Vend becomes a clerk in a grey suit, mortal, four hundred years old, and very tired. What happens to him is the party's choice.
5. **The Signature Line.** The contract is gone, but the Material Plane's title is still ‘Disputed’. The Quill remains. The endings:
   **Tear It Up.** Destroy the Ledger. Every contract it holds dissolves—the good and bad. Chaos, freedom, a lot of confused franchisees.
   **Owned by Nobody.** Write ‘Material Plane: owned by nobody’ and sign as witnesses, not owners. The world is free, and the Ledger becomes a museum piece.
   **Held in Common.** Sign on behalf of every handshake ally (the Commons Tally): the world is owned by everyone who lives in it. The Last Independents become its stewards.
   **New Management.** Sign it themselves. The party owns reality. Run the epilogue with care—they have become Corporate.

Opposition

Tallis Vend, Chairman (see Bestiary), 4 Compliance Officers, 1 Senior Auditor, and whatever the Ledger writes into existence.

Twist

Pip Ashworth—the real one—has followed the party in. He is holding his own QuestRabbit Contractor Agreement. ‘I checked,’ he says. ‘Clause 77-B's in mine too. Does that make me a representative? Can I sign as a witness?’ He can. Any ally present can.

Rewards

The world, in whatever form the party leaves it. Level 20. A five-star rating from someone who means it.

Forward

Epilogue: run one short scene for each franchise the party touched, showing what became of it. End at the Tankard & Tallow, or at THAC0 Spell, at the table where it started.

Receipt

**—**

There are no more receipts. This is where they are spent.

All

Chapter 5

## Corporate Entities

*Creation & Company, QuestRabbit, the ten Board umbrellas and the Last Independents. Every entity is presented in the same dossier format as the franchises it owns: overview, profile, holdings, DM secret (including exactly how its Board seat turns), and contracts.*

Corp. 01

### Creation & Company

*“Everything Is For Sale. Eventually.”*

Parent of All0 franchisesBlack Card[S24](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#m-S24)

Acquire the Material Plane. The Plane's title is Disputed because it has never been sold; a valid transfer needs signatures from trusted representatives of the people who live there.

| Sigil         | Two interlocking C's closing into a ring, like a snake biting a ledger.                                                                                                                       |
| ------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Headquarters  | The Counting House, a skyscraper-sized office outside reality, reachable through the Franchise Below.                                                                                         |
| Public Face   | None. Creation & Company has no public face. Its name appears only in fine print, on survey stones, and at the bottom of the Final Contract.                                                  |
| Controlled By | **Tallis Vend**, Chairman. Four hundred years ago a mortal clerk wrote himself into the Grand Ledger as the owner of himself, in perpetuity, and the Ledger agreed. He has been buying since. |
| Culture       | Silent, patient, polite. Employees of the Counting House speak in minutes and memoranda. Nobody raises their voice. Nobody leaves.                                                            |

#### Holdings & Assets

- **Ten Board Umbrellas.** [Crown & Cleaver Holdings](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#u-cc), [Arcane Beverage International](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#u-abi), [The Restaurant Necromancy Group](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#u-rng), [The Adventure Retail Consortium](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#u-arc), [Probability Brands Group](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#u-pbg), [Nine Circles Hospitality](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#u-nch), [The Planar Hospitality Group](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#u-phg), [The Hoard Syndicate](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#u-hoard), [McGuffin Narrative Holdings](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#u-mnh), [Sweet Covenant Confectionery](https://a33f98a3-9e8c-4971-89d1-989c0440e385.frame.claudeusercontent.com/_f/1790063629-cf0a/#u-scc), plus QuestRabbit (via Human Resources).
- **Assets.** The Grand Ledger; the Quill of Consideration; the Compliance Officers (inevitable-like auditors); the Golden Tusks seal; the Counting House itself.
- **Methods.** Owns ten umbrella corporations through nested shells. Uses QuestRabbit to cultivate trusted heroes. Uses the Grand Ledger to make paperwork into reality.
